Cause: `compliance-gate.yml` fires on `push: branches: [main]` with **no** path filter, so it should have run — but the commit message contained the literal token **`[skip ci]`**, not as an instruction but **inside a quotation describing `5cddf2a3` as "data-only `[skip ci]`"**. GitHub scans the whole message and does not care that the token was quoted. **A commit that merely *mentions* `[skip ci]` skips CI.** So `2419caf3` reached `main` with no gate verdict, and the `#266` observation window it was supposed to test was silently voided. **Standing rule, strengthened: never write the literal token in a refresh commit message — describe an upstream commit as "skip-ci" or "CI-skipped", never with the brackets.** Fixed forward rather than by force-push: this follow-up commit carries a clean message and is the real gate test. **Second method finding:** pushing from a **detached** worktree is rejected by the lane-guard `pre-push` hook (*"branch 'HEAD' does not declare a lane"*) — it reads the **local** ref name, so the worktree must be on a lane-prefixed local branch (used here: `cowork/claudemd-refresh-20260901`) and pushed as `git push origin <branch>:main`. Neither `--no-verify` nor a force-push was used.

Its commit body names a root cause no prior entry had: **this was never a "too many crons" problem.** GitHub bills a **one-minute minimum per job**, the job averages **four seconds**, so 1,440 monthly fires cost 1,440 billed minutes; with Utah's `*/10` equivalent (4,320 runs) that is **~5,760 billed minutes against a 2,000–3,000 allowance — and roughly 88% of the spend was billing-floor overhead, not compute.** The other sixteen schedules total ~200 runs/month combined. **That is what exhausted the quota and tripped the Actions spending limit.** pg_cron calls the same endpoint, from inside the same project, at the same cadence, with the same token — no runner, no floor, no cost. **The multi-week "heartbeat degradation" red is now closed by removal, not by repair:** last GitHub-scheduled `Utah Lead Notification Recovery` run is `33414951560` at **16:35:30Z**, minutes before the merge. > **🟢 (3) THE COMPLIANCE GATE'S BILLING FLOOR AND ITS CONCURRENCY BUG WERE BOTH FIXED TODAY, AND THE SECOND FIX EXISTS BECAUSE A CROSS-LANE AUDIT CAUGHT A FALSE COMMENT IN THE FIRST.** **`48d0b570 ci(compliance-gate): bound every job's runtime and stop paying for superseded runs (#261)`** (merged 18:01:54Z) batches **21 separate jobs into 4** — its own commit body states the arithmetic: GitHub bills Actions **per job, rounded up to a full minute**, so 21 jobs that finished in **109 seconds of wall time were billed 23 minutes**, i.e. ~58% of the Actions bill was pure billing floor. It also bounds every job's runtime (the open-ended stall behind the 8/18 four-hour hang) and cancels superseded runs. Then **`ad196dc7 ci(compliance-gate): stop a queued main run from evicting a pending one (#262)`** (merged 21:16:28Z) corrects #261 in the same day: **Codex's 2026-08-25 cross-lane audit found the comment #261 left in the file — "main is never cancelled" — is false**, because `cancel-in-progress: false` protects only the **running** run, not a **pending** one that a newly queued `main` run can evict. Both landed green: Compliance Gate push run `32900120322` on `ad196dc7` (21:16:31Z, success) and `32913788576` on `7ee6900c` (2026-08-26T00:07:52Z, success). **This is the first window in this log where a fix to CI got audited and corrected by a second PR within three hours.**

> **🔴 (4b) AND THIS REFRESH REPRODUCED THE BUG WHILE DESCRIBING IT — THE TOKEN COUNTS EVEN WHEN IT APPEARS IN PROSE, SO THE 8/20 RULE IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT.** This refresh's first commit **`bcaf4809`** was written deliberately without a skip *directive* — and still produced **0 check-runs on `main`** (`gh api repos/Rconman99/frame-restoration-utah/commits/bcaf4809/check-runs` → `total_count: 0`, re-queried at +8 min). Cause, confirmed against `e80bc3ee`'s own commit body: GitHub scans the **entire commit message** for the skip token, and `bcaf4809`'s body **quoted it literally** inside the sentence asserting compliance (*"a CLAUDE.md refresh commit must NOT carry …"*). `e80bc3ee` had already named this exact failure mode — *"auto-refresh commits had been disabling their own gate **by quoting the literal token in prose**"* — and this refresh walked straight into it five days later. **Corroboration across the log: `b097cacb` (8/20 evening refresh) also shows `total_count: 0`, and it too only mentions the token in prose. `e4ab43cf`, which deliberately omitted every skip form, carries a full 21/21 blocking verdict.** **DURABLE RULE, stronger than the 8/20 version: a commit message on `main` must not contain the skip token in ANY form — not as a directive, not as a quotation, not as an example. Refer to it as "the CI-skip token" or "skip-ci" in prose. The rule applies to the message only; this file may discuss the token freely.** The corrective commit that carries this item was written under that rule, and its verdict is the test.

> **🔴 (2) CORRECTION TO THIS MORNING'S ENTRY — THE LANE GUARD IS *NOT* PASSING ON BOTH OPEN PRs. IT HAS NEVER EVALUATED #260 AT ALL.** The 10:06 UTC refresh wrote: *"The gate is live and passing on both open PRs (`32334918575` on `925b4afd`, `32337548820` on `a3d24db1`)."* **Both of those runs are on the same branch** — `claude/compliance-gate-job-batching-20260819`, i.e. **#261**. Verified three ways: `gh run list --workflow=lane-guard.yml` returns, for its five most recent runs, **four on `claude/compliance-gate-job-batching-20260819` and one on `claude/lane-guard-ci-20260817`** — none on #260's branch; `gh run list --branch=claude/pgcron-lead-worker-20260819` returns **exactly two rows, both stamped 2026-08-19T16:43, neither of them `Lane guard`**; and `gh pr checks 260` returns **24 rows containing ZERO matches for "lane"**, against `gh pr checks 261`'s **25 rows including `Branch declares its lane · pass · 3s`**. **The cause is ordering:** #260's only CI ran **2026-08-19 16:43 UTC**, *before* `lane-guard.yml` merged to `main` as `adbad815`, and **nothing has been pushed to that branch since** (`updatedAt 2026-08-19T16:43:59Z`). **So #260 reads `MERGEABLE`/`CLEAN` on the strength of a check set that predates the gate now required of it** — merging it on that signal merges a branch the lane guard never saw. **A no-op push or a re-run settles it in seconds; do that before merging #260.** Generalized rule for this file: **a green PR check set is only as current as its last run — compare the run timestamp against the merge date of any gate added since.**
> **🔴 (3) SOLVED, AND IT IS SELF-INFLICTED: THIS FILE'S OWN AUTO-REFRESH COMMITS HAVE BEEN SILENTLY DISABLING `main`'s CI BY QUOTING THE LITERAL TOKEN `[skip ci]` INSIDE THEIR PROSE.** The evening refresh first logged this as *"one by design, one unexplained"* and proposed the next `CLAUDE.md` push as the test. **That test ran immediately and failed the same way** — `b097cacb` (the evening refresh commit itself) triggered **zero** workflow runs — which converted a one-off into a reproducible pattern and pointed straight at the cause. **Ruled out first, each by direct inspection:** `compliance-gate.yml`'s trigger is **`push: branches: [main]` with NO `paths:` filter** (the `paths:` list belongs to the `pull_request:` trigger and begins with `'**'`), so path filtering cannot explain it; the workflow file is **unchanged** since `f782e545`; repo Actions permissions read **`{"enabled":true,"allowed_actions":"all"}`**; and the GitHub events API shows **both** `3ef3b02b` (10:10:25Z) and `adbad815` (00:49:14Z) as ordinary `PushEvent`s by the **same actor `Rconman99` to the same ref `refs/heads/main`** — so it is not credentials, not a bot-token push, not repo settings. **The discriminator is the commit message, and the correlation is perfect across twelve consecutive `main` commits:** every commit whose message contains the literal string `[skip ci]` anywhere in its body got **0** `Compliance Gate` push runs — `b097cacb`, `104f2617`, `3ef3b02b`, `201a496c`, `eeaf7d5e` — and every commit without it got **exactly 1** — `adbad815`, `b4cd1127`, `2145ae91`, `dfdfe1c0`, `a4cfdd79`, `e1f2a3c3`. **One exception, and it is separately explained:** `ebfa65c0` has no such token and still got 0, because it was pushed by `github-actions[bot]` — and pushes authenticated with `GITHUB_TOKEN` do not trigger workflows, by documented design. **The mechanism:** GitHub honors the skip token **anywhere in the commit message, not just the subject line** — so a refresh commit that merely *narrates* another commit's status ("…data-only `[skip ci]`", "…no `[skip ci]` this time") skips **its own** CI. **This is why the 8/19 midday entry's `201a496c` also has no verdict**, a gap that entry never noticed about itself. **Consequence: `main` has now gone three consecutive commits without a `Compliance Gate` verdict, and the two the board blamed on `[skip ci]`-by-design were only half the story — the refresh commits were doing it accidentally.** **Fix, and it costs nothing: never write the literal token in a commit message.** Refer to it as *the skip-ci token* in prose, or split it so it cannot match. Same care applies to GitHub's other skip forms (`[ci skip]`, `[no ci]`, `[skip actions]`, `[actions skip]`). **Writing the token inside `CLAUDE.md` itself is harmless — only the commit message is scanned.** **CONFIRMED BY EXPERIMENT:** the follow-up commit `e4ab43cf` deliberately omitted every skip form and **`Compliance Gate` push run `32423554596` fired on it and completed `success` at 22:17:06 UTC** — the first genuine verdict `main`'s tip has carried in four commits. Diagnosis proven: remove the token, CI returns.

> **🟡 (9) THE LANE PROTOCOL IS ALREADY ENFORCED LOCALLY — AND IT BLOCKED THIS REFRESH, BECAUSE THE HOOK HAS A DETACHED-HEAD GAP.** #259 is the *server-side* half; the *client-side* half is already installed at `.git/hooks/pre-push` (from `~/scripts/install-lane-hooks.sh`, per `agentic-context-hub/ACTIVE-LANES.md`). It resolves the lane from **`git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`** and exits 0 only if that name is `main`/`master` or leads with `claude` / `codex` / `cowork` / `agy` / `human`. **In a detached worktree that command returns the literal string `HEAD`** — no lane, not protected — **so the push is rejected even though its destination ref is `main`.** That is exactly how the 8/14–8/17 refreshes have been committing, so **the isolated-worktree pattern this file depends on is now blocked by default.** Today's fix was to satisfy the protocol rather than override it: the worktree was created on a real lane branch, **`cowork/claude-md-refresh-20260818`**, and pushed with `git push origin HEAD:main`. **Recommended follow-up on #259's client-side sibling: resolve the lane from the *destination* ref (the hook already receives it on stdin as `<local-ref> <local-sha> <remote-ref> <remote-sha>`) instead of the local branch name**, so a detached push to a protected ref is judged on where it lands. `--no-verify` was deliberately NOT used.

> **🔴→🟢 (1) THE BIG ONE — `#227` (`73ba3bf`, 09:18 UTC): A CONTROLLED PRODUCTION LEAD PROVED THE PRIMARY AND BACKUP OWNER-NOTIFICATION JOBS COULD BOTH LAND IN THE SAME MAILBOX.** The failure mode: when the live backup-recipient secret happened to equal the primary recipient, the two lanes that exist precisely so one can cover the other silently converged — a single mailbox problem becomes total loss of owner alerting, on the one path that turns organic search into a phone call. **Per the PR body the live secret was already corrected and independently verified before the PR, and the production impact audit found no real customer leads affected** — only the controlled test row, now marked as test data. The code fix makes the collapse structurally impossible rather than operationally unlikely: routes are normalized and **required to be distinct**; durable lead + outbox capture happens **before** routing checks so a bad route can never lose the lead itself; duplicate and stale role-mismatched deliveries are blocked before claim and before provider fetch; ready/expired queues are keyset-paged so **blocked rows cannot starve valid work** out of the 25-ready / 10-expired caps; and a health-only scan of active frozen routes fails closed on mismatch, partial payload, query, cursor, or response-shape errors. Verified with 59 shared Deno tests, 21 owner-notification source-contract tests, 3 intake source tests, and a typecheck of all 11 Edge entrypoints. **Read this as the standing lesson: two lanes are not redundancy until something enforces that they are different.**

> **🟢 (1) THE BIG ONE — `#210` REPLACES THE CLIENT-IP DEPLOY RECEIPT WITH A FAIL-CLOSED v3 GATE IN FRONT OF THE PROTECTED `handle-lead` / `lead-crm` DEPLOY PATH.** This closes the thread the late-night refresh opened when `#205` was built and then contained 49 minutes later by `5f5c799`. What v3 actually does: **separates offline Ed25519 issuance from a public-key-only CI verifier** (CI never holds a signing secret); **binds every receipt to one function, one exact `main` SHA, one project, and one dispatch nonce, with a 15-minute lifetime**; rejects duplicate-key/raw-secret artifacts and stale, reused, cross-function, or drifted receipts; **re-checks the complete live function + secret metadata and probe absence immediately before deploy, inside the same shell step** (closing the TOCTOU window); structurally restricts Production credentials and deploy capability to the exact reviewed workflow; and blocks opaque binary/archive execution plus the reproduced workflow/parser bypass corpus. New in-repo surface: `scripts/audit-client-ip-probe-contract.mjs` (**+2,287**), `scripts/render-client-ip-probe.mjs` (+304), `scripts/issue-client-ip-deploy-receipt.mjs` (+134), `scripts/client-ip-probe-audit-trusted.json`, and a new `supabase/probe-templates/client-ip-probe/index.ts.tmpl` (+175). PR-body verification receipts: **29/29 privileged-CLI attack variants blocked, 33/33 verifier/issuer/renderer/extractor poison variants blocked, 12/12 binary/archive + 5/5 opaque-workflow attacks blocked, 18/18 approved-workflow mutation cases blocked, 114 Deno tests + 24 provider-workflow tests pass, gitleaks clean across all four commits.** `Compliance Gate` green on the PR (`31406699854`, 16:01 UTC) and again on `main` after merge (`31406922891`, 16:03 UTC).

> **🟢 (1) THE BIG ONE — THE GSC QUERY VIEW WAS ~97% BLIND, AND NOW IT ISN'T.** `01d65d6` **#194 "add the GSC page dimension + honest query coverage"** (7 files, +571/−25). The stored query slice covered **2,107 of 85,434 impressions — 2.5%** — and the readout said nothing about it. Fixes: the **page dimension** (`gsc.top_pages`) plus a report-only *"pages earning impressions but zero clicks"* section (query rows structurally cannot answer this — they are a bounded slice, and per-query positions cannot be averaged into a page position); `top_queries` becomes the **union of top 200 by clicks and top 200 by impressions**, because the API only sorts clicks-desc so zero-click queries sank below every 1-click query however many impressions they carried; fetch depth **1000 → 5000**; coverage reported as an **impression share** on the GSC totals line so it renders on a first snapshot. The diff is now **experiment-aware** — candidates whose query *or* page is under a running experiment are held back and reported, never silently dropped. Two porting slips also fixed: the workflow concurrency group read **`seo-loop-texas`**, and **CI never ran the loop's unit tests here**, so a green PR said nothing about the loop. Post-fix coverage is stated honestly in the readout: **392 of 3,184 queries = 58.7% of impressions**.
> **🟢 (2) A ONE-LINE SEAM DEFECT IN THAT SAME PORT WAS CAUGHT AND CLOSED THE SAME DAY.** `724b72d` **#195 "snapshot dropped GSC fields it was not told to copy"** (3 files, +101/−20). `seo-snapshot.mjs` copied GSC fields **one by one** and was never told about `queries_stored_impressions` — *the exact number #194 existed to report*. **Two unit suites passed straight over the gap:** one proved the field was RETURNED, the other injected it DIRECTLY into a synthetic snapshot; neither exercised the seam between them. Fix: **spread instead of enumerate**, so a new field carries through by default rather than by being remembered in a second list; `siteUrl` now persists as provenance (which Search Console property produced the numbers — it matters with several markets on one loop and two property forms); new `seo-snapshot.test.mjs` asserts **every key the client returns reaches `snapshot.gsc`**, so it fails on the NEXT dropped field — verified by reverting the fix against the source market. 34/34 tests.
> **🔴🟢 (3) THE MEASURED VERDICT: THE LOCATION PAGES ARE NOT THE PROBLEM, AND NO TITLE REWRITE FIXES THEM.** `17dc7c6` **#196** ships `docs/seo/UTAH-GROWTH-PLAN.md` (+182, **research-only — no page edited, no public surface touched**). Clicks by page type, 2026-07-08 → 08-04, page dimension (which captures **100%** of clicks, so this is exact): **locations 55 pages / 58,714 impr / 13 clicks / 0.02%**; `/pages/*` services 12 / 22,188 / 2 / **0.01%**; root + GBP-tagged 11 / 15,513 / 37 / 0.24%; **blog 54 / 3,560 / 21 clicks / 0.59%**. **Blog converts 29× better per impression than location pages — and it is the smallest surface.** The cause was verified on live SERPs, not inferred: **7 of 8 city-service SERPs put a local pack above every organic result**, and Frame appears in the pack only near Heber City where the business physically is (Magna, Farmington, Eagle Mountain are 30–60 miles out). The decisive proof is our own content — `blog/alpine/storm-damage-roof-repair-alpine` ranks **position 3.6 with 657 impressions and ZERO clicks** (a page at 3–4 should convert 8–15%) while `blog/utah/utah-roof-ventilation-guide` converts at **2.8% from position 7.1**. The pages themselves are fine: 2,754 words, Article + FAQPage + City + GeoCoordinates schema, clean crawl. **The variable is query type, not page type.**
